Search algorithm plan:

Top-level helpers:
- fast_convolve(a): FFT linear convolution.
- objective_from_conv(a): compute 2*n*max(conv)/sum². But use evaluate_sequence for scoring.
- gradient_smoothed(a, beta): compute c, p=softmax(beta*c), gradient of logsumexp(beta c). Need stable softmax: c - max, exp, sum. Because c length 2n-1. p maybe if beta high, top few. Use FFT for base gradient.
   Let's derive FFT gradient correctly and test with small mentally.
Given c = conv(a,a). Weighted sum L=∑ p_k c_k. Gradient g_i = ∑_k p_k ∂c_k/∂a_i.
We can compute via convolution of p with a reversed as above:
base = cross_corr(p, a) where base_i = ∑_k p_k a_{k-i}, with a_j=0 if j outside [0,n-1].
Then g = base + extra, extra_i = p_{2i} a_i if 0≤2i≤2n-2.
But wait if k-i=i, base includes p_{2i} a_i once. Derivative of term a_i² requires 2, so add once. Good.
Now compute base efficiently:
Let A = a, P = p (length 2n-1). Need vector length n: base_i = ∑_{k=0}^{2n-2} P_k A_{k-i} (where A index valid 0..n-1).
As derived: full = conv(P, A_rev) where A_rev[j]=A[n-1-j]. full length 3n-2. base_i = full[n-1+i]. Let's verify with n=2. a=[a0,a1], Arev=[a1,a0]. P length3 [p0,p1,p2]. conv full:
index0: p0*a1
1: p1*a1 + p0*a0
2: p2*a1 + p1*a0
3: p2*a0
base_0 should p0*a0 + p1*a1? (k=0: p0 a0, k=1: p1 a1, k=2: a2 invalid). full[1]=p1*a1+p0*a0 ok. base_1: k=1 p1 a0, k=2 p2 a1; full[2]=p2*a1+p1*a0 ok.
Extra: i=0 p0*a0? For c0=a0² derivative 2a0. base_0 includes p0 a0; add p0 a0. i=1: 2i=2 p2 a1; base_1 no p2 a1? base_1 includes p2 a1, add p2 a1. Good.
If k odd, no extra. Good.
For gradient of β logsumexp, multiply by β: g = β*(base+extra). But p=exp(β(c-cmax))/Z.
We can compute base via FFT: size = next_power_of_two(len(P)+len(Arev)-1) = next_pow_2(3n-2). Use rfft. full = irfft(...)[:3n-2]. Then base=full[n-1:n+n-1]. Complexity one FFT size ~4n. Good.
But note p length odd, a length n; size 3n maybe. For n=4096 size 16384; fine.

- softmax of c: if beta large, exp underflow ok. c_max. Use scipy.special? Could use np.exp. c values ~1/n, beta maybe up to thousands or millions. If beta too high, p focuses. For optimization, use adaptive beta from e.g. 10/n? Actually c scale ~0.001. To smooth max, β maybe 100*n? Let's define β = np.log(K)/gap? Simpler: β = int(1000*n)? For n=768, c~0.001, β=768000 => βc ~0.8 for typical? Hmm typical c ~0.0005, βc ~384, huge. Need choose β so β*(max - typical) ~ few. The peak excess maybe 0.0001? Need high to focus. Since scale invariant, objective logsumexp adds constant β cmax? But gradient okay.
However gradient of logsumexp of c values ~1/n. Multiplying by β large increases gradient magnitude; step size should adapt. We can normalize gradient by its norm or use beta in step.

Projected gradient on simplex with logsumexp:
Given a simplex. L = logsumexp(β c). grad = β * gradient_c_weighted. We want minimize L over simplex. Since L scale invariant? If scale a by t, L -> log exp(β t² c)+... = t² L + const? Not homogeneous because log. But we keep sum1.
Direction d = grad - np.mean(grad) (to maintain sum). But mean over all n or support? For projection, use d=grad - np.mean(grad). Step η. candidate = project_simplex(a - η d). Backtracking: evaluate objective (actual max or logsumexp) and accept if better (with actual maybe). Use line search fractions [1,0.5,0.25,0.1,0.05,...]. To save time, evaluate actual objective (fast_convolve max) for candidates. But for backtracking need L maybe. Actual objective is what matters.
Momentum: maintain velocity v = momentum v + d? For projected gradient, can use non-smooth? Could use heavy ball on a after projection: v = mom v - lr*grad_centered; a=project(a+v). But need backtracking.

Alternatively, use multiplicative weights (mirror) which may be more natural:
Given gradient g of logsumexp or max subgradient. Update a <- adjust a * exp(-η g), normalize. Since L scale invariant? If g constant, no change. Backtracking possible. This may handle large β. Step size small.
Which converges better? For simplex optimization, multiplicative weights is equivalent to mirror descent with KL; good for positive variables. We can combine: every iteration compute smoothed p and gradient; update both projected and multiplicative, choose better by line search over multipliers.

Let's design a robust local optimizer using "active peak smoothing" with log-sum-exp and multiplicative weights, which should be faster than LP and maybe more effective.

Algorithm `smooth_optimize(a0, time_limit, beta_schedule)`:
- Normalize a0 simplex, n.

Let's implement `logsoftmax_update`:
```
def softmax(v):
    v = v - np.max(v)
    z = np.exp(v)
    return z / z.sum()
```
But if v large range? okay. We can maintain phi = log(a) - log(sum) initially; due normalized, mean log. To prevent overflow, subtract max each step? phi values log ratio; if a min 1e-12, range ~28. Step small. Good.

Gradient function:
```
def smooth_gradient(a, beta):
    c = fast_convolve(a)
    z = beta*(c - c.max())
    # To avoid underflow, if z min too negative, exp okay. sumz
    p = np.exp(z)
    p /= p.sum()
    # base via FFT
    A_rev = a[::-1]
    size = 1 << (3*n - 2 - 1).bit_length() maybe. Use same? full length 3n-2.
    P = np.zeros(size); P[:len(p)] = p
    A = np.zeros(size); A[:n] = A_rev
    full = np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(P)*np.fft.rfft(A), size)[:3*n-2]
    base = full[n-1:n+n-1]
    grad = base.copy()
    # extra p[2i]*a_i
    tw = np.arange(0, 2*n-1, 2)  # length n
    grad += p[tw] * a
    return beta*grad, c
```
Check tw length n: 0 to 2n-2 step2. p length 2n-1. ok.
But fast_convolve uses size 2n-1. For gradient size = next pow >= len(p)+n-1 = (2n-1)+n-1=3n-2. We can precompute size. For n not power, bit_length.
Potential numerical: p sum may be small if beta high? z max 0, at least one exp=1, sum≥1.

For actual objective:
```
def fast_obj(a):
    c=fast_convolve(a)
    s=a.sum()
    if s<=0: return inf
    return 2*len(a)*c.max()/(s*s)
```
Since a normalized sum1, and after softmax sum1. But due normalize after floor? We'll normalize.

Let's design `log_space_optimize` in detail.

Inputs: a0 np array length n, end_time absolute, max_time? Or `time_limit` relative. Use relative simpler: run until time_limit. But also need quick exit. We'll pass `time_limit` and use t0.

Pseudo:
```
def log_space_optimize(a0, time_limit, beta0=None, beta1=None, move_scale=0.03, verbose=False):
    a = np.maximum(a0,0).astype(float)
    s=a.sum()
    if s <=0: a=np.ones(n)/n
    else: a=a/s
    n=len(a)
    eps=1e-15
    a_pos=np.maximum(a, eps/(n+1))? Need preserve sum.
    a_pos = np.maximum(a, 1e-15)
    a_pos = a_pos / a_pos.sum()
    phi = np.log(a_pos)
    # optional remove mean
    phi -= phi.mean()
    beta = n if beta0 is None else beta0
    beta_max = 50*n if beta1 is None else beta1
    v = np.zeros(n)
    mom=0.7
    base_move=move_scale
    best_a=a_pos.copy()
    best_obj=fast_obj(best_a)
    c=fast_convolve(best_a)
    cur_obj=best_obj
    stagnant=0
    it=0
    t0=time.monotonic()
    while time.monotonic()-t0 < time_limit:
       # compute gradient
       z = beta*(c - c.max())
       # clip z for stability
       p=np.exp(z); p/=p.sum()
       grad = a_gradient_weights(a_pos, p) # returns unweighted? include beta outside
       grad *= beta
       h = grad - a_pos*grad.sum()
       h -= h.mean()
       # momentum
       v = mom*v + h
       norm = np.max(np.abs(v))
       if norm < 1e-30: break? perturb
       lr = base_move / (norm + 1e-15)
       best_factor=0.0; best_cand= None; best_c_obj=cur_obj; best_c_j=... 
       # We need current J maybe for acceptance? Use actual.
       for factor in [1.0,0.5,0.25,0.15,0.08,0.04,0.02,0.01]:
           phi_try = phi - lr*factor*v
           phi_try -= phi_try.mean() # avoid drift
           a_try = softmax(phi_try)
           obj_try = fast_obj(a_try)
           if obj_try < best_c_obj - 1e-15:
                best_c_obj=obj_try; best_factor=factor; best_cand=a_try; best_c=fast_convolve(a_try)
       if best_factor>0 and best_cand is not None:
           phi = phi - lr*best_factor*v
           phi -= phi.mean()
           a_pos = softmax(phi) # or best_cand to avoid recompute
           a_pos = best_cand
           c = best_c
           cur_obj = best_c_obj
           # update best
           if cur_obj < best_obj - 1e-15: best_obj=cur_obj; best_a=a_pos.copy(); stagnant=0
           else stagnant+=1
           # if improvement small but accepted, maybe stagnant=0 if log? we use actual.
       else:
           stagnant +=1
           # adapt
           if stagnant % 5 ==0:
               beta = min(beta_max, beta*1.15)
               base_move *= 0.8
               mom = 0.75 if mom<0.85 else 0.65? 
           if base_move < 1e-6:
               # random restart from best
               noise = rng? Need deterministic rng inside? Use np.random.default_rng based on it. No nested.
               phi = np.log(np.maximum(a_pos, eps)) ...
               rng = np.random.default_rng(12345+it)
               phi += rng.standard_normal(n)*(0.01+0.02*min(1,stagnant/100))
               phi -= phi.mean()
               a_pos = softmax(phi); c=fast_convolve(a_pos); cur_obj=fast_obj(a_pos)
               v=np.zeros(n); beta=...; base_move=move_scale; mom=0.7
       if best_obj < global? (cannot update external)
       it+=1
    return best_a, best_obj
```

Parametric candidate generation details:

`resample_sequence(a, new_n)`: use linear interpolation. If parent length 768, resample to new_n. Normalize.

`mirror_sequence(a)`: a[::-1].

Now, can we do a direct local coordinate polish that may produce final 0.002 improvement? Let's design a top-level `peak_polish(a, time_limit)` using deterministic coordinate moves, perhaps faster than gradient at end.

Peak polish idea:
For best_a, maintain conv c. Find top peak k. To reduce c_k, we need adjust a_i for i+j=k. A simple move: choose i in contributing indices with large a_i, move small mass to index l far from k? Better: reduce a_i and increase a_j such that c_k decreases most. The change in c_k for moving δ from p to q is δ[ a_{k-q} - a_{k-p} + a_{q-k} - a_{p-k} ] + O(δ²). To decrease c_k, choose q with small cross terms. If q not in pairs for k, and p pair high, good. But moving mass may increase other peaks. We can evaluate candidate by FFT for top few moves. This is similar previous peak coord. We can improve by trying multiple p/q and exact line search over δ.

Algorithm:
```
def peak_polish(a0, time_limit):
 a=normalize; c,obj=conv_and_obj; best=...
 step_frac=0.02
 while time:
   c,obj=conv_and_obj(a)
   peaks = top 3 indices by c
   improved=False
   for k in peaks:
      js=max(0,k-n+1); je=min(n-1,k)
      # for each p in top contributing pairs by a[p]*a[k-p]
      collect pair values; sort; top 8
      for p in pairs:
        contrib = 2*a[p]*a[k-p] if p==k-p else a[p]*a[k-p]
        if small continue
        # candidate target indices q: choose top 20 random or deterministic far from k and low c contribution
        # maybe all q? n large too many. Choose q with low a? Moving to low a less impact.
        for q in selected_targets:
           if q==p or q==k-p: continue
           # line search δ fractions of a[p]: [0.01,0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2]
           for frac in fracs:
              delta = min(a[p]*frac, a[p])
              cand=a.copy(); cand[p]-=delta; cand[q]+=delta; if cand[p]<0 continue; cand=normalize? If sum unchanged (move) no need normalize if p not negative.
              obj_c=fast_obj(cand)
              if obj_c < best_obj - tol: accept, improved=True; break
        if improved: break
   if not improved:
      # random perturbation of best
      if step_frac>1e-5: step_frac*=0.8
      else break
      if iterations % 100 ==0: candidate=project_simplex(best_a+noise*0.01), evaluate
   # periodically refresh c
 return best
```

Algorithm:
```
def deflation_optimize(a0, time_limit, beta_start, beta_max, tau_base=0.1):
  a=normalize positive
  c=conv; best...
  beta=n*5; tau=tau_base; stagnant=0
  while time:
    p=softmax(beta*c)
    g=base_cross_corr(a,p) # length n nonnegative
    g_mean = g.mean()
    if g_mean < eps: break
    gn = g / g_mean + 1e-12
    # candidate factors tau* [1,0.5,0.25,0.1,0.05]
    for tc in taus:
       a_try = a * (gn ** (-tc))
       # floor, normalize
       a_try = np.maximum(a_try, 0)
       s=a_try.sum(); if s<... continue
       a_try /= s
       obj=fast_obj
       choose best primary obj; secondary J
    if accepted: update a,c,obj; stag=0; maybe tau*=1.05
    else: stag++; if stagnation: beta*=1.2; tau = min(tau*1.1, 2.0) or random perturb
```

def log_gradient_optimize(a0, time_limit, beta_b=None, beta_max_b=None, base_move0=0.12, mom0=0.75, verbose=False):

def add_candidate(cands, a, label, max_count=500):

    if m <=1: return1

`fast_convolve(a)`:
```
n=len(a); size=next_pow2(2*n-1)
A=np.fft.rfft(a,n=size)

`base_cross_corr(a,p)`:
```
n=len(a); full_len=3*n-2; size=next_pow2(full_len)
Ar=np.zeros(size); Ar[:n]=a[::-1]
P=np.zeros(size); P[:len(p)]=p
full=np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(P)*np.fft.rfft(Ar),n=size)[:full_len]
base=full[n-1:n-1+n]
# roundoff negative clip to 0 maybe
return np.maximum(base,0.0)
```

`log_gradient_optimize(a0, end_time, B0=8.0, Bmax=120.0, base_move0=0.12, mom0=0.75, verbose=False)`:
- t_end = end_time
- Initialize.
- While `time.monotonic() < t_end - 0.2`:
   - compute p,J = softmax_weights(c,B)
   - g = base_cross_corr(a,p)
   - gm = g - g.mean()
   - norm = np.max(np.abs(gm)) if gm.size else 0; if norm <1e-15: stagnant++; adapt; if norm tiny too many break
   - v = mom*v + gm
   - v -= v.mean()
   - vnorm = max_abs(v)
   - step = base_move / (vnorm + 1e-15)
   - best_factor=0; best_a_try=None; best_c_try=None; best_obj_try=cur_obj; best_J_try=cur_J; improved=False
   - factors = (1.0,0.6,0.35,0.2,0.1,0.05,0.02)
   - for factor in factors:
       phi_try = phi - factor*step*v
       # optional keep phi not too large: if np.max(np.abs(phi_try))>100? not issue
       a_try = softmax_phi(phi_try)
       a_try = normalize_positive(a_try) # this may add tiny and renormalize; okay but floor cost. Could skip floor if min>1e-20.
       c_try = fast_convolve(a_try)
       obj_try = obj_from_conv(c_try,n)
       J_try = smooth_obj_from_c(c_try, B)
       if obj_try < cur_obj - 1e-14 or (obj_try < cur_obj + 1e-12 and J_try < cur_J - 1e-7): choose
   - if best_a_try is not None:
       update phi, a, c, cur_obj, cur_J
       if cur_obj < best_obj - 1e-14: best_obj=cur_obj; best_a=a.copy(); stagnant=0; # maybe increase base_move slightly? base_move=min(base_move0*2,base_move*1.05)
       else: stagnant+=1
       # adapt base_move if many accepts? Keep.
   else:
       stagnant +=1
       base_move *= 0.85
       B = min(Bmax, B*1.15)
       if stagnant > 40:
          B = max(B0*0.5, B*0.7)
       if stagnant > 100:
          # restart from best with noise
          rng = np.random.default_rng(1000003 + it*7919)

          v=np.zeros(n); B=B0; base_move=base_move0; stagnant=0
       if base_move < 0.003:
          base_move=base_move0*0.2
   # if verbose print

```

Let's code `add_candidate` carefully:
```
def add_candidate(cands, a, label, max_count=500):
    if len(cands) >= max_count: return
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if a.size == 0: return
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
        a[~np.isfinite(a)] = 0.0
    s = a.sum()
    if s <= 1e-15: return
    a = a / s
    cands.append((a, label))
```
No closure.

`make_param_candidates`:
```
def make_param_candidates(n, parent, rng, max_count=80):
    cands=[]
    base_parent = parent if parent is not None else np.ones(n)/n
    # Use add_candidate with max_count
    a0=np.ones(n); add_candidate(cands,a0,'uniform',max_count)
    p=resample_sequence(base_parent,n); add_candidate(cands,p,'parent',max_count)
    add_candidate(cands,p[::-1],'parent_rev',max_count)
    for alpha in [-3.0,-1.0,-0.4,0.4,1.0,3.0]: add_candidate(cands,tilt_sequence(p,alpha),f'tilt{alpha}',max_count)
    add_candidate(cands,smooth_sequence(p,0.01),'smooth1',max_count)
    add_candidate(cands,smooth_sequence(p,0.03),'smooth3',max_count)
    add_candidate(cands,smooth_sequence(p[::-1],0.02),'smoothrev',max_count)
    # noise around parent
    for scale in [0.02,0.05,0.1]:
       noise=rng.standard_normal(n)*scale*np.mean(p)
       add_candidate(cands, p+noise, f'noise{scale}', max_count)
    x=(np.arange(n)+0.5)/n
    # exp
    for k in [-4.0,-2.0,-1.0,1.0,2.0,4.0,6.0]:
       f=np.exp(k*(x-0.5)); add_candidate(cands,f,f'exp{k}',max_count)
    # linear tilts
    for c in [-1.5,-1.0,-0.6,-0.3,0.3,0.6,1.0,1.5]:
       f=1.0 + c*(2*x-1); f=np.maximum(f,1e-6); add_candidate(cands,f,f'lin{c}',max_count)
    # beta
    for alpha in [0.5,0.8,1.0,1.5,2.0,3.0,4.0,6.0]:
       for beta in [0.5,0.8,1.0,1.5,2.0,3.0,4.0,6.0]:
          if len(cands) >= max_count: break
          xe=np.clip(x,1e-6,1-1e-6)
          f=xe**(alpha-1.0)*(1.0-xe)**(beta-1.0)
          add_candidate(cands,f,f'beta{alpha}_{beta}',max_count)
       if len(cands) >= max_count: break
    # Gaussians
    for center in [0.2,0.35,0.5,0.65,0.8]:
       for sig in [0.05,0.1,0.2,0.3]:
          if len(cands) >= max_count: break
          f=np.exp(-0.5*((x-center)/sig)**2)
          add_candidate(cands,f,f'g{center}_{sig}',max_count)
    # sine random
    for _ in range(15):
       if len(cands)>=max_count: break
       f=np.ones(n)
       for m in [1,2,3,4]:
          amp=rng.uniform(0.05,0.7)
          phase=rng.uniform(0.0,2.0*np.pi)
          f = f + amp*np.cos(2.0*np.pi*m*x + phase)
       add_candidate(cands,f,f'sin{...}',max_count)
    # piecewise
    for _ in range(15):
       if len(cands)>=max_count: break
       m=int(rng.integers(3,7))
       breaks=np.sort(rng.uniform(0.0,1.0,m+1)); breaks[0]=0; breaks[-1]=1
       heights=rng.uniform(0.0,2.0,m+1)
       f=np.interp(x,breaks,heights)
       add_candidate(cands,f,f'plinear{...}',max_count)
    # if too few, add random simplex
    while len(cands)<min(max_count, 50):
       f=rng.dirichlet(np.ones(n)*0.3)
       add_candidate(cands,f,'dirichlet',max_count)
    return cands
```
Need labels maybe not unique; not important. Avoid f-strings? Python 3.6 okay. No lambda.
Some loops with large beta 64; max_count maybe 80, so will stop before all. Good.
Potential cost: generating beta for n=3072 64 arrays and exp, okay.

`tilt_sequence`:
```
def tilt_sequence(a, alpha):
    n=len(a); x=np.linspace(0,1,n); w=np.exp(alpha*(x-0.5)*4) # maybe scale
    b=a*w; b=np.maximum(b,0); s=sum; if s>0 return b/s; return a
```
Use alpha scale maybe α in [-3,3] enough.

`smooth_sequence`:
```
def smooth_sequence(a, sigma_frac):
    n=len(a); sigma=max(1.0, sigma_frac*n); rad=int(3*sigma)+1
    if rad <=1: return a.copy()
    if rad > n//2: # use full Gaussian? kernel length n centered? Simpler: use FFT convolution with kernel length n? For rad>=n, use uniform? 
       kernel=np.ones(n); 
    else:
       k=np.arange(-rad, rad+1); kernel=np.exp(-0.5*(k/sigma)**2)
    kern=kernel/kernel.sum()
    b=np.convolve(a, kern[::-1], mode='same')
    b=np.maximum(b,0); s=b.sum(); if s>0: return b/s; return a
```
If rad > n//2, np.convolve with kernel length 2rad+1 >n results output length n+2rad, mode same weird? Actually mode same returns first n output. Better if rad>=n/2, just normalize a (or use uniform kernel length n). Use `kernel=np.ones(n)`.

`resample_sequence` stable:
```
def resample_sequence(a, new_n):
    a=np.asarray(a,float); n_old=len(a)
    if n_old==0: return np.ones(new_n)/new_n
    a=np.maximum(a,0); s=a.sum(); if s<=0: return ones
    if n_old==new_n: return a/s
    x_old=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n_old,endpoint=False)
    x_new=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,new_n,endpoint=False)
    b=np.interp(x_new,x_old,a/s, left=0.0, right=0.0)
    b=np.maximum(b,0); bs=b.sum(); if bs<=0: return ones; return b/bs
```

`score_candidates`:
```
def score_candidates(cands):
    scored=[]
    for a,label in cands:
       obj=fast_obj(a)
       if np.isfinite(obj): scored.append((float(obj), a, label))
    scored.sort(key=score_key)
    return scored
def score_key(t): return t[0]
```

Now, details of `log_gradient_optimize`: If `a0` has length n, and `base_cross_corr` maybe slow. We can precompute? no.

Let's write `log_gradient_optimize` code carefully to avoid variables unbound.

```
def log_gradient_optimize(a0, end_time, B0=25.0, Bmax=250.0, base_move0=0.12, mom0=0.75, verbose=False):
    a = np.asarray(a0, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    n = a.size
    a = np.maximum(a,0.0)
    s = a.sum()
    if s <= 1e-300:
        a = np.ones(n)/n
    else:
        a = a/s
    tiny = 1e-20
    a = np.maximum(a, tiny)
    a = a / a.sum()
    phi = np.log(a)
    phi -= phi.mean()
    c = fast_convolve(a)
    cur_obj = float(2.0*n*c.max())
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_obj = cur_obj
    B = B0
    base_move = base_move0
    v = np.zeros(n)
    stagnant = 0
    it = 0
    _, cur_J = softmax_weights(c, B)
    while time.monotonic() < end_time - 0.3:
        # compute p
        p, cur_J = softmax_weights(c, B)
        g = base_cross_corr(a, p)
        gm = g - g.mean()
        gmax = float(np.max(np.abs(gm)))
        if not np.isfinite(gmax) or gmax <= 1e-15:
            stagnant += 1
            if stagnant > 50:
                break
            continue
        v = mom0*v + gm
        v = v - v.mean()
        vmax = float(np.max(np.abs(v)))
        if not np.isfinite(vmax) or vmax <= 1e-15:
            stagnant += 1
            if stagnant > 50: break
            continue
        step = base_move / (vmax + 1e-15)
        best_factor = 0.0
        best_phi = None
        best_a_trial = None
        best_c_trial = None
        best_obj_trial = cur_obj
        best_J_trial = cur_J
        factors = (1.0, 0.6, 0.35, 0.2, 0.1, 0.05, 0.02)
        for factor in factors:
            phi_try = phi - factor * step * v
            # avoid huge phi shift? maybe cap
            if np.max(np.abs(phi_try)) > 200.0:
                # rescale? skip? probably not happen
                continue
            a_trial = softmax_phi(phi_try)
            a_trial = normalize_positive(a_trial)
            c_trial = fast_convolve(a_trial)
            obj_trial = float(2.0*n*c_trial.max())
            J_trial = smooth_obj_from_c(c_trial, B)
            if (obj_trial < cur_obj - 1e-14) or (obj_trial < cur_obj + 1e-12 and J_trial < cur_J - 1e-8):
                best_factor = factor
                best_phi = phi_try
                best_a_trial = a_trial
                best_c_trial = c_trial
                best_obj_trial = obj_trial
                best_J_trial = J_trial
                # Could break if factor=1 and obj much lower? Not necessarily smaller factor may better, continue all.
        if best_factor > 0.0:
            phi = best_phi
            a = best_a_trial
            c = best_c_trial
            cur_obj = best_obj_trial
            cur_J = best_J_trial
            if cur_obj < best_obj - 1e-14:
                best_obj = cur_obj
                best_a = a.copy()
                stagnant = 0
                base_move = min(base_move0*3.0, base_move*1.05)
            else:
                stagnant += 1
                base_move = max(base_move0*0.5, base_move*0.9)
        else:
            stagnant += 1
            base_move = min(1.0, base_move * 1.2)
            B = min(Bmax, B * 1.08)
            if stagnant > 60:
                B = max(8.0, B * 0.75)
            if stagnant % 25 == 0:
                # small shuffle from best
                rng = np.random.default_rng(97 + it*104729)
                scale = 0.05 + 0.20*min(1.0, stagnant/150.0)
                phi_pert = np.log(np.maximum(best_a, tiny))
                phi_pert = phi_pert + rng.standard_normal(n) * scale
                phi_pert = phi_pert - phi_pert.mean()
                a_pert = softmax_phi(phi_pert)
                a_pert = normalize_positive(a_pert)
                c_pert = fast_convolve(a_pert)
                obj_pert = float(2.0*n*c_pert.max())
                if obj_pert < best_obj:  # if perturbation itself better? update current? maybe set current to perturbation regardless to explore
                    best_obj = obj_pert
                    best_a = a_pert.copy()
                # set current to perturbation (even if worse than best) for exploration
                phi = phi_pert
                a = a_pert
                c = c_pert
                cur_obj = obj_pert
                _, cur_J = softmax_weights(c, B)
                v = np.zeros(n)
                B = max(10.0, B*0.5)
                base_move = base_move0
                stagnant = 0
        if verbose and it % 200 == 0:
            print(f"    log_opt it={it}, best={best_obj:.10f}, cur={cur_obj:.10f}, B={B:.2f}, move={base_move:.4f}, stag={stagnant}")
        it += 1
        if stagnant > 3000:
            break
    # ensure best_a normalized
    best_a = normalize_positive(best_a)
    return best_a, float(2.0 * len(best_a) * fast_convolve(best_a).max())
```
This might perform random perturbation even if not improving, which can be beneficial but may waste. It sets current to perturbation and continues. If perturbation very bad, algorithm may spend time. Scale moderate.

We need `normalize_positive` top-level:
```
def normalize_positive(a, tiny=1e-20):
   a=np.asarray(a,float); a=np.maximum(a,0)
   s=a.sum()
   if s<=1e-300: return np.ones(a.size)/a.size
   a=a/s
   if a.size>0:
      a=np.maximum(a, tiny)
      a=a/a.sum()
   return a
```

`smooth_obj_from_c(c,B)`:
```
def smooth_obj_from_c(c, B):
    cmax=float(c.max())
    if cmax <= 1e-300: return float(B)
    z=B*(c-cmax)/cmax
    z=np.clip(z,-745.0,0.0)
    s=np.exp(z).sum()
    if s<=0: s=1.0
    return float(B + np.log(s))
```
`softmax_weights` similar returns p,J. Could call smooth_obj.

Potential issue: `cur_J` after update from line search uses candidate c but p computed from old c? Acceptance criterion J_trial computed with same B and candidate c, good. After accepting, next iteration recomputes p from new c, cur_J from that. In update block, we set cur_J=best_J_trial which is candidate's smooth objective. Good.

Now, `zero_polish` implementation with `fast_obj` maybe returns inf if sum zero. It should keep best if zero all? Not.
```
def zero_polish(a0, end_time):
   a=normalize_positive(a0)
   best=a.copy(); best_obj=fast_obj(a)
   order=np.argsort(a)
   max_checks=min(300, len(a))
   for idx in order[:max_checks]:
      if time.monotonic() > end_time - 0.5: break
      if a[idx] <= 1e-12: # skip if already tiny? but best may not have? we use a current
          continue
      cand=best.copy()
      cand[idx]=0.0
      s=cand.sum()
      if s<=1e-15: continue
      cand=cand/s
      obj=fast_obj(cand)
      if np.isfinite(obj) and obj < best_obj - 1e-15:
          best=cand; best_obj=obj; a=best.copy()
   return best
```

Maybe optimize `base_cross_corr` by using `np.fft.rfft(a[::-1], size)` and `np.fft.rfft(p, size)` where p length <size, rfft zero pads automatically! You don't need prezero. `np.fft.rfft(p, n=size)` zero pads. Good. Use:
```
def base_cross_corr(a,p):
   n=len(a); full_len=3*n-2; size=next_pow2(full_len)
   A = np.fft.rfft(a[::-1], n=size)
   P = np.fft.rfft(p, n=size)
   full = np.fft.irfft(A*P, n=size)[:full_len]
   base=full[n-1:n-1+n]
   return np.maximum(base,0.0)
```
This avoids allocating zero arrays, rfft internally zero pads? It does. Good. For p length 2n-1, size ~4n; efficient.

```python
"""
Search for low-peak non-autocorrelation step functions.

This module minimizes
    2*n*max(convolve(a,a)) / sum(a)^2
for non-negative sequences.  The main optimizer is a smoothed gradient method
in log-space:
  - p_k is a softmax over the convolution peaks (adaptive temperature),
  - g = cross-correlation(a, p) is the gradient of weighted peaks,
  - phi = log(a) is updated by projected/multiplicative-weight steps,
  - adaptive temperature, step size, momentum, and restarts are used.

Multiple sequence lengths and diverse parametric starts are evaluated quickly,
then the most promising candidates are polished with the log-space optimizer.
"""

import time
import numpy as np


def next_pow2(m):
    """Smallest power of two >= m."""
    m = int(max(1, m))
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()


def fast_convolve(a):
    """Linear auto-convolution via FFT, with tiny negative roundoff clipped."""
    n = len(a)
    out_len = 2 * n - 1
    size = next_pow2(out_len)
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, n=size)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A * A, n=size)[:out_len]
    # Mathematically nonnegative; clip numerical noise.
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    return c


def base_cross_corr(a, p):
    """
    Return base_i = sum_k p_k a_{k-i}, with invalid a indices ignored.
    This is the unweighted half of the gradient of sum_k p_k (a*a)_k.
    """
    n = len(a)
    full_len = 3 * n - 2
    size = next_pow2(full_len)
    A = np.fft.rfft(a[::-1], n=size)
    P = np.fft.rfft(p, n=size)
    full = np.fft.irfft(A * P, n=size)[:full_len]
    base = full[n - 1:n + n - 1]
    base = np.maximum(base, 0.0)
    return base


def softmax_weights(c, B):
    """
    p_k proportional to exp(B*(c_k-cmax)/cmax).
    B is a dimensionless temperature: exp(-B) down-weighting relative to peak.
    Returns p and smooth objective B + log(sum exp z).
    """
    cmax = float(c.max())
    if cmax <= 1e-300:
        m = len(c)
        return np.ones(m, dtype=np.float64) / m, float(B)
    z = B * (c - cmax) / cmax
    z = np.clip(z, -745.0, 0.0)
    w = np.exp(z)
    s = float(w.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        w = np.ones(len(c), dtype=np.float64)
        s = float(w.size)
    w = w / s
    J = float(B + np.log(s))
    return w, J


def smooth_obj_from_c(c, B):
    """Smooth objective for a convolution c and dimensionless temperature B."""
    cmax = float(c.max())
    if cmax <= 1e-300:
        return float(B)
    z = B * (c - cmax) / cmax
    z = np.clip(z, -745.0, 0.0)
    s = float(np.exp(z).sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        s = 1.0
    return float(B + np.log(s))


def softmax_phi(phi):
    """Stable softmax of unconstrained log-weights."""
    phi = np.asarray(phi, dtype=np.float64)
    if phi.size == 0:
        return np.ones(0, dtype=np.float64)
    m = float(phi.max())
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return np.ones(phi.size, dtype=np.float64) / phi.size
    z = np.exp(phi - m)
    s = float(z.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.ones(phi.size, dtype=np.float64) / phi.size
    return z / s


def normalize_positive(a, tiny=1e-20):
    """Clip to nonnegative, normalize to sum 1, and keep strictly positive."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    if a.size == 0:
        return a
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64) / a.size
    a = a / s
    a = np.maximum(a, tiny)
    a = a / a.sum()
    return a


def obj_from_conv(c, n):
    """Objective for a normalized sequence from its convolution."""
    return float(2.0 * n * c.max())


def fast_obj(a):
    """Fast objective equivalent to evaluate_sequence for valid normalized input."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return float("inf")
    a = a / s
    n = len(a)
    c = fast_convolve(a)
    return obj_from_conv(c, n)


def score_key(entry):
    """Sort key for (score, ...) tuples."""
    return entry[0]


def resample_sequence(a, new_n):
    """Linearly resample a nonnegative sequence to a new length."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    old_n = len(a)
    if old_n == 0:
        return np.ones(int(new_n), dtype=np.float64) / int(new_n)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.ones(int(new_n), dtype=np.float64) / int(new_n)
    a = a / s
    new_n = int(new_n)
    if old_n == new_n:
        return a.copy()
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, old_n, endpoint=False)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, new_n, endpoint=False)
    b = np.interp(x_new, x_old, a, left=a[0], right=a[-1])
    b = np.maximum(b, 0.0)
    bs = float(b.sum())
    if bs <= 1e-300:
        return np.ones(new_n, dtype=np.float64) / new_n
    return b / bs


def tilt_sequence(a, alpha):
    """Multiply by an exponential tilt and normalize."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = len(a)
    if n == 0:
        return a
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    w = np.exp(4.0 * alpha * (x - 0.5))
    b = a * w
    b = np.maximum(b, 0.0)
    s = float(b.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return a / a.sum()
    return b / s


def smooth_sequence(a, sigma_frac):
    """Gaussian smoothing, approximately preserving sum."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = len(a)
    if n == 0:
        return a
    sigma = max(1.0, sigma_frac * n)
    rad = int(3 * sigma) + 1
    if rad <= 1:
        return a.copy()
    if rad >= n // 2:
        kernel = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        k = np.arange(-rad, rad + 1, dtype=np.float64)
        kernel = np.exp(-0.5 * (k / sigma) ** 2)
    kernel = kernel / kernel.sum()
    b = np.convolve(a, kernel[::-1], mode="same")
    b = np.maximum(b, 0.0)
    s = float(b.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return a / a.sum()
    return b / s


def add_candidate(cands, a, label, max_count=500):
    """Add a valid nonnegative normalized candidate to a list."""
    if len(cands) >= int(max_count):
        return
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if a.size == 0:
        return
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
        a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return
    a = a / s
    cands.append((a, label))


def make_param_candidates(n, base_parent, rng, max_count=80):
    """Generate diverse smooth nonnegative starts of length n."""
    n = int(n)
    cands = []
    max_count = int(max_count)
    if base_parent is None:
        base_parent = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / n
    p = resample_sequence(base_parent, n)

    add_candidate(cands, np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64), "uniform", max_count)
    add_candidate(cands, p, "base", max_count)
    add_candidate(cands, p[::-1], "base_rev", max_count)

    for alpha in (-3.0, -1.0, -0.4, 0.4, 1.0, 3.0):
        add_candidate(cands, tilt_sequence(p, alpha), "tilt", max_count)

    add_candidate(cands, smooth_sequence(p, 0.008), "smooth_lo", max_count)
    add_candidate(cands, smooth_sequence(p, 0.025), "smooth_md", max_count)
    add_candidate(cands, smooth_sequence(p[::-1], 0.018), "smooth_rev", max_count)

    mp = float(p.mean())
    for scale in (0.02, 0.05, 0.10):
        noise = rng.standard_normal(n) * (scale * mp)
        add_candidate(cands, p + noise, "noise", max_count)

    x = (np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5) / n

    for k in (-4.0, -2.0, -1.0, 1.0, 2.0, 4.0, 6.0):
        f = np.exp(k * (x - 0.5))
        add_candidate(cands, f, "exp", max_count)

    for c in (-1.5, -1.0, -0.6, -0.3, 0.3, 0.6, 1.0, 1.5):
        f = 1.0 + c * (2.0 * x - 1.0)
        f = np.maximum(f, 1e-6)
        add_candidate(cands, f, "lin", max_count)

    # Beta-like densities.
    for alpha in (0.5, 0.8, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 3.0, 4.0, 6.0):
        if len(cands) >= max_count:
            break
        for beta in (0.5, 0.8, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 3.0, 4.0, 6.0):
            if len(cands) >= max_count:
                break
            xe = np.clip(x, 1e-6, 1.0 - 1e-6)
            f = xe ** (alpha - 1.0) * (1.0 - xe) ** (beta - 1.0)
            add_candidate(cands, f, "beta", max_count)

    # Gaussian bumps.
    for center in (0.20, 0.35, 0.50, 0.65, 0.80):
        if len(cands) >= max_count:
            break
        for sig in (0.05, 0.10, 0.20, 0.30):
            if len(cands) >= max_count:
                break
            f = np.exp(-0.5 * ((x - center) / sig) ** 2)
            add_candidate(cands, f, "gauss", max_count)

    # Random low-order Fourier nonnegative shapes.
    for _ in range(18):
        if len(cands) >= max_count:
            break
        f = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        for m in (1, 2, 3, 4):
            amp = rng.uniform(0.05, 0.70)
            phase = rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi)
            f = f + amp * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * m * x + phase)
        add_candidate(cands, f, "fourier", max_count)

    # Random piecewise-linear shapes.
    for _ in range(18):
        if len(cands) >= max_count:
            break
        m = int(rng.integers(3, 7))
        breaks = np.sort(rng.uniform(0.0, 1.0, m + 1))
        breaks[0] = 0.0
        breaks[-1] = 1.0
        heights = rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0, m + 1)
        f = np.interp(x, breaks, heights)
        add_candidate(cands, f, "plinear", max_count)

    # Fill with random Dirichlet if needed.
    while len(cands) < min(max_count, 60):
        f = rng.dirichlet(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) * 0.3)
        add_candidate(cands, f, "dirichlet", max_count)

    return cands


def score_candidates(cands):
    """Score candidates and return list sorted by objective."""
    scored = []
    for a, label in cands:
        obj = fast_obj(a)
        if np.isfinite(obj):
            scored.append((float(obj), a, label))
    scored.sort(key=score_key)
    return scored


def log_gradient_optimize(a0, end_time, B0=25.0, Bmax=250.0,
                          base_move0=0.12, mom0=0.75, verbose=False):
    """
    Smoothed multiplicative-weight log-search for a single sequence length.

    a0 : initial nonnegative sequence
    end_time : absolute time limit (time.monotonic())
    """
    a = np.asarray(a0, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    n = len(a)
    if n == 0:
        return np.ones(0, dtype=np.float64), float("inf")

    a = normalize_positive(a)
    phi = np.log(np.maximum(a, 1e-300))
    phi -= phi.mean()

    c = fast_convolve(a)
    cur_obj = obj_from_conv(c, n)
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_obj = cur_obj

    B = float(B0)
    base_move = float(base_move0)
    v = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    stagnant = 0
    it = 0
    _, cur_J = softmax_weights(c, B)

    factors = (1.0, 0.6, 0.35, 0.2, 0.1, 0.05, 0.02)

    while time.monotonic() < end_time - 0.3:
        p, cur_J = softmax_weights(c, B)
        g = base_cross_corr(a, p)
        gm = g - g.mean()
        gmax = float(np.max(np.abs(gm))) if gm.size else 0.0

        vmax = 0.0
        if np.isfinite(gmax) and gmax > 1e-15:
            v = mom0 * v + gm
            v = v - v.mean()
            vmax = float(np.max(np.abs(v))) if v.size else 0.0

        best_factor = 0.0
        best_phi = None
        best_a_trial = None
        best_c_trial = None
        best_obj_trial = cur_obj
        best_J_trial = cur_J

        if np.isfinite(vmax) and vmax > 1e-15:
            step = base_move / (vmax + 1e-15)
            for factor in factors:
                phi_try = phi - factor * step * v
                if not np.all(np.isfinite(phi_try)):
                    continue
                # Avoid absurd log-range growth.
                if float(np.max(np.abs(phi_try))) > 300.0:
                    continue
                a_trial = softmax_phi(phi_try)
                a_trial = normalize_positive(a_trial)
                c_trial = fast_convolve(a_trial)
                obj_trial = obj_from_conv(c_trial, n)
                J_trial = smooth_obj_from_c(c_trial, B)
                if (obj_trial < cur_obj - 1e-14) or (
                        obj_trial < cur_obj + 1e-12 and J_trial < cur_J - 1e-8):
                    best_factor = factor
                    best_phi = phi_try
                    best_a_trial = a_trial
                    best_c_trial = c_trial
                    best_obj_trial = obj_trial
                    best_J_trial = J_trial

        if best_factor > 0.0 and best_phi is not None:
            a = best_a_trial
            c = best_c_trial
            cur_obj = best_obj_trial
            cur_J = best_J_trial
            phi = np.log(np.maximum(a, 1e-300))
            phi -= phi.mean()

            if cur_obj < best_obj - 1e-14:
                best_obj = cur_obj
                best_a = a.copy()
                stagnant = 0
                base_move = min(base_move0 * 3.0, base_move * 1.05)
            else:
                stagnant += 1
                base_move = max(base_move0 * 0.5, base_move * 0.9)
        else:
            stagnant += 1
            base_move = min(1.0, base_move * 1.18)
            B = min(float(Bmax), B * 1.08)
            if stagnant > 60:
                B = max(8.0, B * 0.75)

            if stagnant % 25 == 0:
                rng = np.random.default_rng(97 + it * 104729)
                scale = 0.05 + 0.20 * min(1.0, stagnant / 150.0)
                phi_pert = np.log(np.maximum(best_a, 1e-300))
                phi_pert = phi_pert + rng.standard_normal(n) * scale
                phi_pert = phi_pert - phi_pert.mean()
                a_pert = softmax_phi(phi_pert)
                a_pert = normalize_positive(a_pert)
                c_pert = fast_convolve(a_pert)
                obj_pert = obj_from_conv(c_pert, n)
                if obj_pert < best_obj:
                    best_obj = obj_pert
                    best_a = a_pert.copy()
                phi = phi_pert
                a = a_pert
                c = c_pert
                cur_obj = obj_pert
                _, cur_J = softmax_weights(c, B)
                v = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
                B = max(10.0, B * 0.5)
                base_move = float(base_move0)
                stagnant = 0

        if verbose and it % 200 == 0:
            print(f"    log_opt it={it}, best={best_obj:.10f}, cur={cur_obj:.10f}, "
                  f"B={B:.2f}, move={base_move:.4f}, stag={stagnant}")

        it += 1
        if stagnant > 5000:
            break

    best_a = normalize_positive(best_a)
    final_obj = fast_obj(best_a)
    return best_a, float(final_obj)


def zero_polish(a0, end_time):
    """Try zeroing very small coordinates if it improves the objective."""
    a = normalize_positive(a0)
    best = a.copy()
    best_obj = fast_obj(a)
    if not np.isfinite(best_obj):
        return best

    order = np.argsort(a)
    max_checks = min(300, len(a))
    for idx in order[:max_checks]:
        if time.monotonic() > end_time - 0.5:
            break
        if best[idx] <= 1e-13:
            continue
        cand = best.copy()
        cand[idx] = 0.0
        s = float(cand.sum())
        if s <= 1e-300:
            continue
        cand = cand / s
        obj = fast_obj(cand)
        if np.isfinite(obj) and obj < best_obj - 1e-15:
            best = cand
            best_obj = obj
    return best


def finalize_sequence(a):
    """Return a plain Python list of finite nonnegative floats summing to 1."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0:
        return [1.0]
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return [1.0]
    a = a / s
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s2 = float(a.sum())
    if s2 <= 1e-300:
        return [1.0]
    a = a / s2
    return [float(x) for x in a]


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800):
    """
    Main entry point. Search for a sequence minimizing evaluate_sequence.
    Must return a list of nonnegative floats. """
    t_start = time.monotonic()
    total_budget = max(60.0, float(budget_s))
    # Reserve time for final verification and clean return.
    end_time = t_start + total_budget - 60.0
    if end_time < t_start + 30.0:
        end_time = t_start + total_budget

    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))

    best_seq = None
    best_score = float("inf")

    # Load previous construction if available.
    parent = None
    try:
        raw_parent = height_sequence_1  # noqa: F821
        p = np.asarray(raw_parent, dtype=np.float64)
        if p.ndim == 1 and p.size > 0 and np.all(np.isfinite(p)):
            p = np.clip(p, 0.0, 1000.0)
            if p.sum() > 1e-15:
                parent = p / p.sum()
                if parent.size > 20000:
                    parent = resample_sequence(parent, 4096)
                ps = fast_obj(parent)
                if np.isfinite(ps):
                    best_seq = parent.copy()
                    best_score = float(ps)
                    print(f"Parent loaded: n={len(parent)}, fast_score={ps:.10f}")
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"Could not load parent: {e}")

    if best_seq is None:
        best_seq = np.ones(512, dtype=np.float64) / 512.0
        best_score = fast_obj(best_seq)
        print(f"Initialized with uniform n={len(best_seq)}, score={best_score:.10f}")

    # Quick safe resampling of the current best to several lengths.
    init_ns = [max(256, int(best_seq.size)), 384, 512, 768, 1024, 1536, 2048, 3072, 4096]
    init_ns = sorted(set(n for n in init_ns if 256 <= n <= 4096))
    init_end = min(end_time, t_start + 30.0)
    seen_init = set()
    for n in init_ns:
        if time.monotonic() > init_end:
            break
        if n in seen_init:
            continue
        seen_init.add(n)
        a = resample_sequence(best_seq, n)
        obj = fast_obj(a)
        if np.isfinite(obj) and obj < best_score:
            best_score = obj
            best_seq = a.copy()
            print(f"Resample improved: n={n}, score={obj:.10f}")

    # Rapid parameter scan over several lengths.
    scan_lengths = [384, 512, 768, 1024, 1536, 2048, 3072, 4096]
    if int(best_seq.size) not in scan_lengths:
        scan_lengths.append(int(best_seq.size))
    scan_lengths = sorted(set(n for n in scan_lengths if 256 <= n <= 4096))

    scan_budget = min(240.0, max(60.0, total_budget * 0.12))
    scan_end = min(end_time, t_start + scan_budget)
    best_by_n = {}
    scan_base = parent if parent is not None else best_seq

    for n in scan_lengths:
        if time.monotonic() > scan_end - 5.0:
            break
        if n <= 1024:
            maxc = 70
        elif n <= 2048:
            maxc = 50
        else:
            maxc = 35
        cands = make_param_candidates(n, scan_base, rng, max_count=maxc)
        # Always allow the current best to compete at this length.
        add_candidate(cands, resample_sequence(best_seq, n), "current_best_resample", max_count + 3)

        scored = score_candidates(cands)
        if scored:
            obj, a, label = scored[0]
            if obj < best_score:
                best_score = float(obj)
                best_seq = a.copy()
                scan_base = best_seq.copy()
                print(f"Scan improve: n={n}, score={obj:.10f}, type={label}")
            best_by_n[n] = (a.copy(), float(obj))
        else:
            a = resample_sequence(best_seq, n)
            obj = fast_obj(a)
            best_by_n[n] = (a.copy(), float(obj))

    # Build per-length candidates for deeper optimization.
    candidate_by_n = {}
    n0 = int(best_seq.size)
    s0 = fast_obj(best_seq)
    candidate_by_n[n0] = (best_seq.copy(), float(s0))

    for n, (a, s) in best_by_n.items():
        old = candidate_by_n.get(n)
        if old is None or s < old[1]:
            candidate_by_n[n] = (a.copy(), float(s))

    for n in (1024, 1536, 2048, 3072, 4096):
        if n < 256:
            continue
        a = resample_sequence(best_seq, n)
        s = fast_obj(a)
        old = candidate_by_n.get(n)
        if old is None or s < old[1]:
            candidate_by_n[n] = (a.copy(), float(s))

    target_entries = [(s, n) for n, (a, s) in candidate_by_n.items()]
    target_entries.sort(key=score_key)
    target_ns = [n for s, n in target_entries[:6]]

    if not target_ns:
        target_ns = [n0]

    # Allocate optimization time.  Later targets can be regenerated from the
    # best sequence found so far inside the loop.
    total_opt_time = max(60.0, end_time - time.monotonic() - 120.0)
    weights = []
    total_w = 0.0
    for idx, n in enumerate(target_ns):
        w = 1.0 + 0.35 * (float(n) / 2048.0)
        if idx == 0:
            w *= 1.7
        weights.append(w)
        total_w += w

    for idx, n in enumerate(target_ns):
        if time.monotonic() >= end_time - 120.0:
            break

        cand_a, cand_s = candidate_by_n[n]
        res_a = resample_sequence(best_seq, n)
        res_s = fast_obj(res_a)

        if res_s <= cand_s:
            a_start = res_a
        else:
            a_start = cand_a.copy()

        desired = total_opt_time * weights[idx] / total_w
        if idx == len(target_ns) - 1:
            desired = max(10.0, end_time - 120.0 - time.monotonic())
        desired = max(20.0, min(900.0, desired))
        call_end = min(time.monotonic() + desired, end_time - 120.0)
        if call_end - time.monotonic() < 10.0:
            break

        a_opt, obj_opt = log_gradient_optimize(
            a_start,
            call_end,
            B0=25.0,
            Bmax=250.0,
            base_move0=0.12,
            mom0=0.75,
            verbose=False,
        )

        s_opt = fast_obj(a_opt)
        old = candidate_by_n.get(n)
        if old is None or s_opt < old[1]:
            candidate_by_n[n] = (a_opt.copy(), float(s_opt))

        if np.isfinite(s_opt) and s_opt < best_score:
            best_score = float(s_opt)
            best_seq = a_opt.copy()
            print(f"Deep optimize improve: n={n}, score={s_opt:.10f}")

    # Final polish on the best sequence found so far.
    if time.monotonic() < end_time - 20.0 and best_seq is not None:
        a_opt, obj_opt = log_gradient_optimize(
            best_seq,
            end_time - 10.0,
            B0=35.0,
            Bmax=300.0,
            base_move0=0.05,
            mom0=0.65,
            verbose=False,
        )
        s_opt = fast_obj(a_opt)
        if np.isfinite(s_opt) and s_opt < best_score:
            best_score = float(s_opt)
            best_seq = a_opt.copy()
            print(f"Final polish improve: n={len(best_seq)}, score={s_opt:.10f}")

    # Very small coordinate zeroing can occasionally help.
    if time.monotonic() < end_time - 3.0 and best_seq is not None:
        best_seq = zero_polish(best_seq, end_time)
        zscore = fast_obj(best_seq)
        if np.isfinite(zscore) and zscore < best_score:
            best_score = float(zscore)

    # Final normalization and evaluation.
    final_list = finalize_sequence(best_seq)
    try:
        final_score = float(evaluate_sequence(final_list))
    except Exception:
        final_score = float("inf")

    if not np.isfinite(final_score):
        final_list = [1.0]
        final_score = float(evaluate_sequence(final_list))

    print(f"FINAL selected fast score: {best_score:.10f}, "
          f"evaluate_sequence(final): {final_score:.10f}, n={len(final_list)}")
    return final_list
```
